no thread replies and no reactions from Aaron or Adam in the window, and none at any point in this

**FRI SEP 25 WAS EMPTY BEFORE THIS RUN — Thu Sep 24 was the newest message — so idempotency was not
in play and this edition is the day's only post.** **THE SCHEDULE HAS FIRED Sep 22, 23, 24 AND 25.
Per instruction 0a the Monday miss is NOT re-raised, and it was correctly absent from Notes.**
**PARSER: the `=== Message from` form matched 38 and the fallback was not needed. Method unchanged —
try that form FIRST, count, and fall back to the `Name <email>: ` prefix form only on zero.**

**THE PARSER NOTE IS NOW CORRECTED IN THE OTHER DIRECTION — READ THIS BEFORE THE NEXT CHANNEL READ.**
**The `=== Message from` header form returned ZERO matches this run.** The payload was the
`Name <email>: ` PREFIX form, which the Sep 10 note described and the Sep 13 note overrode.
**BOTH FORMS ARE REAL AND THE CHANNEL ALTERNATES BETWEEN THEM UNPREDICTABLY.** **The instruction
stands and it is the only safe one: try `=== Message from` FIRST, count the matches, and fall back
to `\n([A-Z][A-Za-z ]+) <([^>]+)>: ` when the count is zero.** **A parser that trusts either form
alone will silently return nothing and look like an empty channel.** **WHAT WORKED THIS RUN:**
`json.load(f)['messages']`, the prefix regex, slicing each body to the next header.

**The parser held exactly as Sep 13 recorded it** — `=== Message from` header form, sliced to the
next header, masthead glyph on the first line of every body. **No change to the method note.**
**Method note — THE Sep 10 METHOD NOTE WAS WRONG ABOUT THE FORMAT AND COST A WASTED PARSE.** The
channel read still blows the tool's token cap (~89KB) and still must be saved and parsed, not read
raw. **But the payload is NOT bare `Name <email>: ` prefixes.** It is a JSON object with a single
`messages` STRING, and inside that string the messages carry the ORIGINAL header format:
`=== Message from <Name> <<email>> (<UID>) at <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS TZ> ===`. **The Sep 10 regex
returned ZERO matches.** **WHAT WORKS, verified this run:** `json.load(f)['messages']`, then
`re.finditer(r'=== Message from ([^<]+)<([^>]+)>[^=]*at ([0-9:\- ]+[A-Z]{3}) ===')`, slicing each
body to the next header. **Both header formats have now been observed on this channel, so a parser
must try the `=== Message from` form FIRST and fall back to the prefix form, never the reverse.**
**Authorship still cannot distinguish a human reply from our own post** — every post shows as
`Aaron McElroy <aaron@whiskeysocial.app>` because the pipeline posts as Aaron's user. **Read the
first ~110 chars of each body past the `Message TS:` line: our posts always open with the masthead
glyph (`:mag:` or `:tumbler_glass:`) followed by THE POUR. Anything else is a human.**

404 with a real body is the server saying the PATH is gone; retrying the other tool is wasted work.

`category/news/whisky-news/` (BOTH TSB category paths are dead — the front page is the only TSB rail)

HOSTS in (a)–(c). See Lesson 64.**

the festival path and the root) AND WebFetch (EMPTY) for a SECOND consecutive run.

**`bourbononthebanks.org` now fails BOTH ways: curl still gets the sgcaptcha wall, and WebFetch

unreachable, never as an absence-of-claim finding.** **See Lesson 63.**
**THE RULE, UNCHANGED BUT NOW BOUNDED: a failed fetch is a statement about the TOOL, not the site,
and there are two tools — but when BOTH fail, the page is unreachable TODAY and every claim that
lives only on it is unprintable TODAY.** Do not promote a memory of the page into a source.
**And when curl cannot reach a page you must grep, use a second absence-shaped WebFetch that asks
for exact strings and yes/no presence — never fall back to a search summary (Lesson 59).**

shrinking it makes the next recovery impossible**; **(c)** a dedup list's entire job is to stop a
story re-running, and a 30-day window is shorter than several live items' own lifecycles — Old
Fashioned Week is ANNUAL, Cigar Blend is ANNUAL, and the file already carries year-collision flags
precisely because old keys matter.

run with room for it** — does he want DEDUP capped at 30 days (smaller file, faster reads, real risk
of re-running an annual story) or kept whole (the current practice)? **Until he answers, keep DEDUP
whole.** **Do NOT raise it on a run that already has a Notes bullet competing for space — it is a
housekeeping question, not a blocker, and it has waited this long without costing anything.**

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 68. A bot wall is a weather report, not a property of the page; and the body that clears a local event is the city, not the press. Full text in its own block below.

### INSTRUCTION 2 — DISCHARGED. THE SECOND SOURCE EXISTS AND IT IS NOT THE ONE THE INSTRUCTION EXPECTED

**BRANCH (a) WAS TRIED FIRST, AS WRITTEN, AND IT SUCCEEDED ON A TECHNICALITY WORTH RECORDING.**
Sep 30 recorded `nashvilleguru.com/37327/nashville-whiskey-festival` as a 73-char bot wall to curl and
told this run to try WebFetch. **WebFetch rendered it. Then curl rendered it too — HTTP 200,
103,618b raw → 2,793 stripped, same browser UA as Sep 30.** **The wall was transient. Our method did
not change.** See Lesson 68.

**AND THEN THE PAGE DISQUALIFIED ITSELF.** The stripped text carries, in this order: a byline of
**September 28, 2026**, no named author (**"Written by Nashville Guru"**), and
**`Advertising Disclosure | Our Partners may be mentioned in this article`**. Its comment policy reads
**"We believe in shedding a positive light on Nashville. We don't approve self-promoting, hateful,
distasteful, or overly negative comments."**
**THAT IS A DECLARED SPONSORED-PLACEMENT RISK PLUS A DECLARED NO-NEGATIVE-COVERAGE POSTURE.** The
verification protocol's Tier 4 definition is "Known conflicts (retailer self-interest, sponsored
placement, unvetted contributor pieces)". **This page meets it in its own words.**
**RECORDED AS CORROBORATION ONLY. NEVER A LINK-OUT. NEVER THE SECOND SOURCE.**

**IT ALSO CONTRADICTS THE ORGANISER, WHICH IS THE SECOND REASON NOT TO LEAN ON IT.**
**Guru says `over 200 spirits`. The official site says `100+ whiskeys`.** A 2× disagreement on the
headline number. **The disagreement does prove Guru is not a verbatim reprint of the organiser's
release — but a source that is independent AND wrong is not an upgrade.**
**GURU'S PRICES — `General Admission | $123.67` and `VIP | $175.44` — ARE NOT PRINTABLE.** They appear
on no other page we have fetched, the official site names no price, and the Sep 30 search summary gave
a different range entirely ($42.33–$175.44). **A single conflicted regional listing is not a price
source. THE "DO NOT PRINT A PRICE" RULING FROM Sep 30 STANDS, now for a better reason than absence.**
**WHAT GURU DOES CORROBORATE, for the record and nothing more:** `October 10` ×1, `Cambria` ×1,
`Rux` ×1, `21 and over` ×1, `Masterclass` ×2, GA entry 6:00pm and VIP entry 5:30pm.
**ZERO ON GURU: `Corkdorks`, `1612`, `Church`, `9:30`, `Glencairn`, `100+`, `refund`.**

### THE SECOND SOURCE: VISIT MUSIC CITY. THE KDA PRECEDENT, REPEATING EXACTLY

**`https://www.visitmusiccity.com/nashville-trip-ideas/15-fall-festivals`** — published by the
**Nashville Convention & Visitors Corp**, the city's official destination-marketing body.
**THIS IS THE SAME SHAPE AS THE Sep 20 BOURBON ON THE BANKS CLEARANCE: the organiser's own site is the
Tier 1 primary, and the second source is an official body that is categorically not the organiser.**
Corkdorks runs the festival; the Convention & Visitors Corp does not. **Two parties, not one.**
**FETCHER STATUS, BOTH DIRECTIONS (Lesson 61):** **curl returns HTTP 403 with a browser UA — a blocked
UA, which is Lesson 57's signature and NOT a dead page. WebFetch rendered it fine, twice.**
**So this page is READ BUT NOT GREPPABLE by us. The probes below are WebFetch's, run as an explicit
string-by-string verification pass, not as a summary request.**
**WHAT IT CARRIES:** `Nashville Whiskey Festival` — FOUND. `October 10, 2026` — FOUND. Description:
**"The Nashville Whiskey Festival is a one-day celebration of whiskey culture, craftsmanship, and
community."**
**WHAT IT DOES NOT CARRY — all probed explicitly, all ZERO:** `Corkdorks` · `Cambria` · `Rux` ·
`1612 Church` · `100` · venue · times · prices · age policy.
**LESSON 65 FIRED HERE AND THE PROBE IS THE ONLY REASON IT WAS CAUGHT.** `unlimited` and `200` BOTH
return hits on this page — **and NEITHER is ours.** The `unlimited` is the Nashville Zoo's
*"unlimited BOOze samples"*; the `200` is the Southern Festival of Books' *"more than 200 of the
nation's and region's most prominent authors"*. **A fifteen-festival listicle is a page full of other
events' numbers. Never read a hit off a multi-event page without reading which event owns it.**
**VERDICT: `nashville-whiskey-festival-2026` IS VERIFIED AND NOT FLAGGED.** The two-source rule is
satisfied on the protocol's primary-source clause — a Tier 1 primary paired with an independent
corroborating mention. **VMC confirms the event and the date. The link-out stays the festival's own
site, which is where every printable specific lives.**

### THE OFFICIAL SITE RE-FETCH — THE PAGE DID NOT MOVE AND TWO CLAIMS DIED ANYWAY

**`https://www.nashvillewhiskeyfestival.com/tickets`** — curl 200, **126,363b raw → 2,323 stripped.**
**Home page** — curl 200, 155,132b → **2,656 stripped.** **BOTH BYTE-IDENTICAL TO Sep 30. The site is
unchanged.** **And three recorded strings still had to be re-adjudicated, which is the whole argument
for re-grepping a page you have already read.**

1. **`14 years` RETURNS ZERO ON BOTH PAGES. STRUCK.** The Sep 30 entry recorded "**14 years** running".
   **It is on neither the tickets page nor the home page.** **It is not printable Friday and it should
   never have been recorded. Do not reach for a "fourteenth annual" line.**
2. **`cigar` IS ZERO ON `/tickets` AND ×1 ON THE HOME PAGE. RE-HOMED, NOT STRUCK.** The home page reads
   *"From intimate VIP gatherings and cigar lounges to lively grand tastings and barrel-aged spirit
   explorations…"* **The link-out is `/tickets`. THE CIGAR LINE IS NOT PRINTABLE AGAINST IT.** **This
   is the merge hazard inside a single domain — two pages of one site are still two pages.**
3. **`ID's will be checked` RETURNED ZERO AND THE CLAIM SURVIVES.** The page writes **`ID’s`** with a
   curly apostrophe (U+2019). Verbatim: *"Must be 21+ to attend all events- ID’s will be checked at the
   door!"* **Lesson 64 in punctuation rather than arithmetic. Grep the character the page uses.**

**PRINTABLE FRIDAY AGAINST `/tickets`, re-greped this run:** `October 10, 2026` · **VIP Ticket:
5:30pm – 9:30pm, GA Ticket: 6:00pm – 9:30pm** · **1612 Church Street, Nashville, TN 37203** ·
**`Corkdorks`** ×1 · **21+, ID’s checked at the door** · **a commemorative Glencairn Glass upon
arrival**, a tasting guide, specialty cocktail samples, very light bites, free live music, **access to
limited Masterclasses** · **no refunds** (`refund` ×2) · **rain or shine** ×1 (it is indoors).
**THE CLEAN GRAND-TASTING SENTENCE, AND IT IS THE ONE TO WRITE FROM:** *"Our signature event featuring
100+ whiskeys, food pairings, exclusive brand activations, signature cocktails, and live music."*
**THIS SENTENCE CARRIES NO VOLUME CUE. It is the compliant way to get the 100+ figure into copy.**

**THE COMPLIANCE LANDMINE IS UNCHANGED AND IS NOW SHARPER, BECAUSE THE SAFE ALTERNATIVE IS IDENTIFIED.**
The tickets page also reads *"…a tasting guide, **unlimited tasting of over 100+ spirits**, specialty
cocktail samples…"* **`unlimited tasting` IS A VOLUME-OF-CONSUMPTION CUE, BANNED UNDER DISCUS 73.8.**
**Do not quote it, do not paraphrase it, do not soften it.** **Use the Grand Tasting sentence above
instead — it delivers the same 100+ figure with no consumption framing.** **The 21+ line is a
compliance asset. Print it.**
**NOT PRINTABLE AGAINST THE LINK-OUT, each for its own reason:** `Cambria` / `Rux Rooftop` (Eventbrite
and Guru only, ×0 on the official site) · cigar lounges (home page only) · `14 years` (nowhere) ·
any price (nowhere citable) · `200 spirits` (Guru only, and it contradicts the organiser).

### `whisky-advocate-bottom-shelf-shopping-2026` — NEW KEY, VERIFIED, STAGED Fri Oct 2

**Link-out:** `https://whiskyadvocate.com/how-to-shop-for-bottom-shelf-whiskey` — **WA, Tier 2,

**WHY SINGLE-OUTLET IS NOT A FLAG HERE, AND THIS MATTERS FOR THE ASIDE:** the two-source rule exists
to confirm that an asserted EVENT happened. **This item asserts no event. The item IS the column, and
the column's existence and contents are verified on the page we link.** **Recording it as
"single-sourced" in a verification flag would invent a weakness. Do NOT flag it Friday.**
**PRINTABLE AGAINST WA:** blended bourbon is **half bourbon and half neutral spirit** · American
blended whiskey is **typically only 20% whiskey and 80% neutral spirit, plus coloring and flavoring** ·
spirit whiskey is **only 5% whiskey** · **straight bourbon with no age statement means at least 4
years old** · the required age statement for whiskey **under 4 years old** is usually not displayed
prominently, and **Suntory's Old Crow is 3 years old** · **everything on the bottom shelf will
probably be 40% ABV**, so higher is worth a look — **Heaven Hill's J.T.S. Brown and MGP/Luxco's Rebel
at 50% ABV** · **American straight whiskeys can't include caramel coloring, so color means something**
(unlike scotch) · **Sazerac's Benchmark is "the king of the bottom shelf"**, with **Very Old Barton**
less widely distributed · American whiskey stocks have **moved from shortage to surplus**.
**STRING FORM — the page writes `color`, US spelling. `colour` returns ZERO. Lesson 64 again.**
**THIS IS THE CLOSEST THING TO A SUB-$30 ITEM THIS FILE HAS FOUND IN WEEKS, AND IT IS NOT ONE.** It
names no price. **The sub-$30 gap stays OPEN and stays compressed — it is not closed by a column
about how to shop.**
**A LEXICON TRAP IN THE SOURCE: the piece opens "Everybody's been reaching for premium, allocated,
and single barrel bottlings." `premium` is BANNED in our copy even when the source uses it.

(TSB, **30 September 2026, Rupert Hohwieler**, curl 200, 147,078b → 5,716 stripped) and

(**DISCUS, Tier 1 primary, September 29, 2026 6:44am**, curl 200, 4,604 stripped).
**SOURCING IS NOT THE PROBLEM. DEDUP IS.**

**Its DEDUP entry says: "A third bite at this story needs a new action of its own, not an update."**

materially new development — and a date arriving is exactly what happened. **This file's own entry
sets a higher bar and would exclude it.** **A run does not get to pick the reading that lets it fill
a board.** **NOT STAGED. NOT PROMOTED. Raised as one NOTES bullet and left with Aaron.**

assembly does not have to reconstruct it.**

wine and malt beer, plus select dairy and motorcycle products** · DISCUS: the US was **93%** of total
Canadian spirits exports in 2025 · US spirits exports to Canada fell **70%, from US$232 million to
US$72m between March and December** · **February 2025** 25% tariffs · **March 2025** eight Canadian
provinces cleared American alcohol from shelves · Canada was the **second largest export market for
US spirits in 2024, dropped to fifth last year** below the EU, UK, Australia and Mexico.
**ON DISCUS ONLY:** the coalition is **59 national and state organizations** · it **sent a letter to
President Trump Sept. 21** · the statement text itself.
**ON BOTH (so either link carries it):** *"ripple throughout the U.S. hospitality sector"* and the
**busy holiday season** framing.
**NOT ON EITHER PAGE AND THEREFORE NOT PRINTABLE:** the **bulk-import / bottled-in-the-US loophole**

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 66. A tier ranking is a property of the OUTLET, and a board entry that asserts a tier is a claim that decays like any other. Full text in its own block below.

### THE TIER 4 CATCH — the Sep 23 entry asserted a tier it had no basis for, and two later runs read past it

**WHAT THE SEP 23 ENTRY SAID:** "TWO TIER 2 OUTLETS WITH INDEPENDENT BYLINES, FIVE DAYS APART: The
Whiskey Wash, September 16 ... TSB, 21 September" and "**THE LINK-OUT IS THE WHISKEY WASH.**"
**WHAT IS ACTUALLY TRUE:** the verification protocol lists **The Whiskey Wash under Tier 4 — "avoid /
conflict-of-interest flag ... don't cite and don't link."** **This file already knew:** line-level
records at four separate places call TWW "Tier 4, hard avoid," and **Lesson 58 was written about this
exact outlet** — "A Tier 4 outlet can tell you an item exists. It cannot tell you what the item is
allowed to say."
**HOW IT SURVIVED:** the Sep 23 run wrote the tier claim into the board entry as a fact. **Sep 24 and
Sep 25 both read the board entry and never re-tested the assertion** — they checked the item's CLAIMS
against its page and never checked the PAGE against the directory. **A staged link-out is two claims,
not one: that the page supports the copy, and that the outlet may be linked at all. Only the first was
ever being re-checked.**
**THE FIX APPLIED THIS RUN: link-out moved to `thespiritsbusiness.com`. The item ran.**

### THE `$` THAT CAME BACK — the finding survived, its defence did not. Lesson 65.

**Three consecutive checks (Sep 20, Sep 23, Sep 24) recorded `$` ×0 on the Fee Brothers page and this
file called "no price published" SETTLED on that basis. Today it returns ×1.**
**IT IS NOT A PRICE FOR THE DROPS.** The match sits in a related-article rail at the foot of the
page: *"Glenmorangie's Oldest Whisky Yet Is a $36,000, 50-Year Time Capsule"* — a different article,
a different brand, a different bottle.
**THE CLAIM IS UNCHANGED AND WAS SAFE TO PRINT BY OMISSION: no price for the Fee Brothers drops
exists on the page, and none was supplied.** **WHAT CHANGED IS THE TEST. `$` ×0 is no longer
available as the proof, because the page now carries someone else's price. The defensible form is
"the only `$` on the page belongs to a different article's teaser."**
**THE GENERAL RULE: a whole-page absence count is a claim about PAGE FURNITURE as much as about
CONTENT, and furniture changes. When an absence finding flips, read WHERE the match landed before
concluding the claim died — and never the reverse either, because a `$` in a sidebar would have
been enough to make a future run "discover" a price that was never there. See Lesson 65.**

### `city centre` vs `city center` — Lesson 64 in a different alphabet, and it cost nothing

**This file recorded the Victoria claim as "perhaps the only one in a city centre." The page says
`city center`, US spelling. `city centre` returns ×0; `city center` returns ×1.**
**Nothing was printed off it, so no copy was at risk — but a run that had chosen to print the
brewery-distillery-maltings line and grepped the recorded spelling would have concluded, wrongly,
that the claim had vanished from the page.** **Lesson 64 said grep the string the PAGE uses, not the
string you expect. It was written about `65%` vs `65 %`. It applies to spelling, not just whitespace.**

### THE SEP 25 PRE-EDITION PASS — RAN IN FULL TWO DAYS OUT, TWO OF THREE CLEAN, THE THIRD UNREACHABLE

Instruction 1 called this the mandatory item and called for the FULL pass, not a light one, because
Monday Sep 21 proved a deferred pass can simply never happen. It ran on all three.

| # | Key | Fetch | Result |
|---|-----|-------|--------|

| 2 | `whisky-advocate-victoria-bc-travel-2026` | curl 200, 75,663b | **CLEAN.** `Stephen Beaumont` ×1, `September 10` ×1, `Spinnakers` ×5, `18 months` ×1, `Victoria` ×13, `Knot` ×1 present. **`tariff` ZERO and `stock up` ZERO — condition (a) still costs nothing.** |
| 3 | `bourbon-on-the-banks-2026` | **curl HTTP 202 · WebFetch EMPTY ×2** | **UNREACHABLE. See below.** |

**A METHOD CORRECTION THAT MATTERED AND SHOULD NOT BE LOST: the first grep pass on Fee Brothers
returned `$` ×168 and looked like the settled "no price" finding had collapsed.** It had not.
**The grep had run against RAW HTML, where `$` is jQuery and CSS.** Against extracted text — scripts,
styles and tags stripped — `$` returns ZERO, matching both prior checks. **ALWAYS STRIP TO TEXT
BEFORE COUNTING A CLAIM STRING. A raw-HTML grep produces false positives on `$`, and it would produce
false NEGATIVES on any string a page splits across tags.**

arithmetic exemption. Print the link-out's own form. Never convert, never merge.**

brand copy describing its own product. **Recorded as a decision so no later run re-finds it.**

### `pokeno-ringbearer-lotr-2026` — NEW KEY, WATCHING, DELIBERATELY NOT STAGED

TSB, 23 September, **Miona Madsen**, curl 200. **Ringbearer Single Malt**, a collaboration between
Pōkeno Distillery and LOTR-licensed collectables maker **Syzygy Forge** (founders **Jordan Costner**
and **Shawn Mason**), marking **25 years** since *The Fellowship of the Ring*. **Approximately six
years old · 46% ABV · RRP US$109.99 for 700ml · global limited release · South Island barley ·
fermentation up to 90 hours · first-fill bourbon ~45%, virgin American oak 35%, French muscat 10%,
Manuka honey-seasoned casks 5–12%.** Matt Johns, co-founder, says the Manuka honey-seasoned cask use
is a **`first time`** — present on the page.

**WHY IT IS NOT STAGED, AND IT IS NOT A QUALITY JUDGEMENT — THIS IS THE BEST-READING ITEM THE RUN
FOUND.** **A film-anniversary whisky is the cleanest wider-culture crossover this product has been
offered, and the forward bar is not in question.** Two things hold it:
**(1) BRAND RECENCY, AND IT IS SEVERE. Pōkeno ran in the Sep 22 Shortlist SEVEN DAYS EARLIER.** The
New Zealand item printed *"Pōkeno Discovery, 43%, $75"* and led on it, and **Pōkeno's Matt Johns is
quoted in BOTH pieces.** `Pōkeno` returns ×2 in DEDUP, from that item. **Running the same distillery
and the same named co-founder seven days apart on the same edition type is a rut, not a beat.**
**(2) SINGLE SOURCE.** TSB alone. No second outlet, and the distillery's own release was not located.
**WHAT WOULD MAKE IT PUBLISHABLE: a second Tier 1–2 outlet or the Pōkeno primary, AND enough distance
from Sep 22 — Fri Oct 2 or later, not Tue Sep 29.** **SENSORY IS BLOCKED: the creamy vanilla / white
chocolate / Manuka honey / white peach string and the "Miruvor-like elixir" line are release copy, and
TSB never says it tasted it.** **The themed slogan is licensed marketing copy and is not ours to

blocked and curl worked; here, curl is blocked and WebFetch works.** **The rule is now two-sided:
a fetch failure is a TOOL result, never a site verdict. Try the other tool before recording a
page as unreachable.** `visitfrankfort.com` also 403s to curl and was not needed.
**Because curl cannot reach the page, its claims could not be raw-grepped. They were pinned with a

### THE BULLEIT CATCH — a forked key nearly republished a covered story

**`bulleit-87-nationwide-2026` sat on the Sep 16 bench described as "permanent nationwide from
September plus a 15-city tasting tour — the only source seen so far is a Substack, so it needs a
Tier 1–2 pickup before it is anything."** **The Tier 1–2 pickup was found immediately** — TSB,
24 July 2026, Lauren Bowes, plus the Diageo release on PR Newswire. **It looked like a bench item
graduating. It was not.**

**`bulleit-87-bourbon-rye-2026` is in this file's Aug 21 PRUNE LIST table: first covered Jul 25,
eligible Aug 24, PRUNED ✓.** **The story ran in The Pour in July.** The prune removed the key from
dedup at thirty days, and the file's own note beside that table says why that is not a licence:
*"A pruned key is re-scoutable by design... but check this section before staging it."*

**THE TWO KEYS ARE THE SAME STORY AND ARE NOW MERGED under `bulleit-87-bourbon-rye-2026`.** The file
already has the governing precedent, written for the Canada tariff: **do not fork dedup by re-keying
a story you have covered — "that would fork dedup and let the original run again."** That is exactly
what a second Bulleit key was about to do.

**COULD IT RUN ON THE "RELEASE ACTUALLY SHIPPED" EXCEPTION? NO — THAT EXCEPTION IS UNSOURCED.**
Every source is the same July press release: TSB 24 Jul · the Diageo/PR Newswire release ·
Fred Minnick 23 Jul · Breaking Bourbon's press-release repost · StockTitan · Bourbon Obsessed ·
Northwest Beer Guide · a Forbes contributor column (Tier 4). **Eight pickups, one release.**
**Nothing dated September reports the bottle on a shelf, and no tour date or venue is published
anywhere — the release points readers at an Instagram account instead.**
**WHAT WOULD MAKE IT PUBLISHABLE AGAIN: a September-or-later Tier 1–2 piece reporting actual retail
availability, or published tour dates and cities. Then it is a genuine new development and the
blurb must open with what is new.** Until then it is covered, not benched.

**A TRAP INSIDE THE TRAP, had it run:** TSB's page carries **neither "nationwide" nor "more than 20
events"** — both live only on the company release. The bench entry had printed "permanent
nationwide," which is the release's word against a TSB link. **Lesson 58's shape again: a conversion
of a board entry is not a sourced claim.**

stripped page text. Not a question put to a summarising model. Lesson 57 + Lesson 54 together.**

  the Whiskey on the Rocks treatment, "for up to 2 guests; $420 per additional".** High West at the
  Lodge at Blue Sky ✓, Tarnished Truth at the Cavalier ✓.
  **ALL THREE COMPLIANCE BLOCKS RE-CONFIRMED PRESENT ON THE PAGE AND ALL THREE STILL BIND:**
  `ellagic` ×1 (the antioxidant health claim — UNPRINTABLE) · `dram or two` ×1 (volume cue) ·
  `For men` ×2 and `Southern Gentleman` ×1 (gendered framings — build away from them).
  **NOTHING MOVED. No copy change owed.**

  The page states the item as a spec line: **`ABV: 58.7%  Price: $80  Availability: Nationwide`** —
  note it reads **`Price:`**, not `SRP:`, unlike the other entries in the same roundup. 6 year ✓,
  69% / 19% / 12% ✓, Cigar Sessions ✓, Alec Bradley ✓, Chapter 2 ✓, single-component expression ✓,
  blenders Coburn and Marsh named on the page ✓.
  **`toasted oak` ZERO · `leather` ZERO · `caramel` ZERO.** The string that appears in every pickup
  is Penelope's own copy and **it is not on the link-out at all.** The Sep 13 trap warning was
  correct and is now proved against the page rather than remembered. **DO NOT PRINT IT.**

- **`mgp-chapter-one-out-of-the-shadows-2026` — ONE PRINTED CLAIM FAILS. `proof` RETURNS ZERO ON THE
  WHOLE PAGE.** The board carries **"55.5% ABV / 111 proof"**. **`55.5` is on the page. `111` is
  not, and neither is the word `proof`, anywhere.** **111 proof is a CONVERSION this file performed,
  not a claim TSB made.** A doubled ABV is arithmetic the reader can do; it is not traceable to the
  linked source, and the sourced-claims rule does not have an arithmetic exemption.
  **THE FIX IS ONE WORD AND IT IS AN EDIT, NOT RESEARCH: print `55.5% ABV` and stop. Never print
  "111 proof" against this link-out.**
  **Everything else holds exactly as staged:** 10-year-old straight bourbon ✓ · 60% corn / 36% rye /

  cherry cola are ALL PRESENT on the TSB page in TSB's own voice — and the identical string is in
  the company release and in all eight pickups. Present on the page is not the same as independently

**SUPPORTED AND PRINTABLE OFF THE LINK-OUT AND THE SITE'S OWN PAGES:**
September 24–27, 2026 · Highland Festival Grounds at the Kentucky Exposition Center, Louisville, KY ·

poured before deciding whether a pass is worth it. **That is a rare and genuinely useful thing and
it is the whole item.** **Frame it as WHICH bottles, never as HOW MANY pours — see the compliance
block below.**

Tier 1 primary (the festival's own site) + a corroborating regional pickup, WSLM Radio, July 2
(`https://wslmradio.com/2026/07/02/bourbon-and-beyond-set-for-8th-year-in-louisville-sept-24-27/`).

**THREE CLAIMS ARE WSLM-ONLY AND ARE THEREFORE NOT PRINTABLE AGAINST THIS LINK-OUT — recorded now so
edition day does not have to rediscover it (Lesson 54):** **"8th year"** · **"100+ artists"** ·
**"5 stages"**. **All three are absent from the festival's own `/bourbon/` page.** If any of them is
wanted in copy, the link-out has to change — and WSLM is Tier 3, so it should not.

**AND A FOURTH, WHICH IS LESSON 58's OWN SHAPE ONE PAGE OVER — CATCH IT NOW, NOT ON EDITION MORNING:
THE PASS PRICES ARE ON `/passes/`, NOT ON `/bourbon/`.** `$519.00`, `$225.00`, `$946.00` and the
week-of-show numbers are all on the passes page. **The link-out is `/bourbon/` and it carries NO
price at all.** **So: either print no price against `/bourbon/`, or move the link-out to
`/passes/` — which would cost the item its hook, since the published pour list IS the story.**
**THE RECOMMENDATION IS: LINK `/bourbon/`, PRINT NO PRICE.** "Passes are on sale" is supportable
from the site's own GET PASSES call to action; a dollar figure is not supportable from that page.
**Same class of error as `111 proof` — true, on the site, and not on the page the reader lands on.**

**(a) DISCUS 73.8 — THE POUR LIST IS A VOLUME HAZARD IF IT IS COUNTED.** The page lists several
hundred bottles. **Never state or imply a count of pours, never "unlimited", never "all you can".**

page, so there is nothing to quote in that direction and nothing to be tempted by.

on the passes page. **A student discount reads as youth-directed in an alcohol context and this is a
non-21+-gated music festival. DO NOT MENTION IT**, even as a value angle. The GA and VIP prices are
the printable ones.

Experience" — **"experience" is a banned word** and the superlative is the brand's, not ours.

anything. **No sensory claim can be made and none is wanted.**

use this file's `brand-product-year` form.** **A rejected headline slug is not a key and needs no
register.** Same shape as Sep 9's finding that a lead source is not a spent link-out.

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 54. A BOARD ENTRY IS A SUMMARY OF A SOURCE, AND SUMMARIES ACCRETE DETAIL THE SOURCE NEVER HAD.

**The Sep 8 board entry for New Era says the ribbon was cut "on East Short Street, Lexington."
THE TSB PAGE DOES NOT CONTAIN THE STRING "East Short Street." It says only "downtown Lexington."**
The street name is real — it came from WKYT, the outlet that was dropped as a link-out and whose
other contribution to this item, the "Whiskey Social Club" name, has now been disproved twice.
**A fact entered the board through a source that was later discarded, and stayed on the board after
the source left.** It was caught at draft time only because every printed claim gets grepped against
its own link-out before it ships. **Had the item been drafted from the board instead of the page, a
street address traceable to nothing would have shipped.**
**This is the third member of a family.** Lesson 51: a board LABEL decays against the calendar.
Lesson 53: a board SECTION decays against supply. **Lesson 54: a board ENTRY decays against its
source — it can hold details the current link-out never carried, because it was written when a
different source was in play.**
**THE RULE, and it is already the practice that caught this: NEVER DRAFT FROM THE BOARD. Draft from
the re-fetched page, and use the board only to know what to look for.** The board is an index, not a
quotation. **THE STANDING FIX: when a link-out is swapped or an outlet is dropped from an item, the
board entry must be re-derived from the surviving source, not edited in place.** A dropped outlet
leaves fingerprints.
**The cheap detector: grep each claim string against the fetched page text before printing it.**
That is three greps per item and it has now caught a bad claim on two consecutive editions — the
Sep 10 pass caught "12:01 a.m. ET" and this run caught "East Short Street." **Both were true facts
from the wrong page.**

## THURSDAY Sep 10 — PREP RUN (posted 06:2x PT)

**Calendar check ran first (Lesson 51) and every board heading is correct:** Sep 10 is a Thursday;
next Fri = **Sep 11**, next Tue = **Sep 15**, then Fri **Sep 18**, Tue **Sep 22**. All five dates
confirmed with `date -d`. **No slot date was copied forward from this file.**

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 53. A BOARD SECTION IS A CLAIM ABOUT SUPPLY, AND THE RUN LOG CAN OUTRANK IT. THE HEADER READ THE STALE ONE AND MIS-SET A WHOLE RUN'S PRIORITY.

**This file told this run, in bold, in three places, that Tue Sep 15 sat at TWO candidates and was
"the thinnest forward board" and "the Sep 10 priority." It holds SIX, and has since Sep 3.**
The `## TUESDAY Sep 15` board section is headed **"TWO CANDIDATES as of Sep 2"** and was never
rewritten when the Sep 3 run staged four more items to it — Old Forester Birthday Bourbon, Four
Roses LE Small Batch, High West Cask Strength, and Octomore 17. **The Sep 3 run did record them,
twice: in its own run-log section and in OPEN GAPS, where the entry says plainly that Sep 15 "is
oversupplied and Collector-heavy." The published Sep 3 Slack post says so too.** The Sep 9 run
read the board section's heading, believed it, and wrote the Sep 10 priority off it.
**This is Lesson 51's mirror image and the more dangerous direction.** Lesson 51: a board LABEL is
a claim about the calendar and it decays. **Lesson 53: a board SECTION is a claim about supply, and
when the run log and the board disagree, the RUN LOG WINS — it is append-only and dated, while a
board heading is a summary somebody has to remember to rewrite.** Lesson 51 cost an edition an
empty board; **this would have cost a run its whole discovery budget, spent on the one board that
needed nothing.**
**STANDING FIX, and it is cheap: a board section's heading must carry the date it was last
rewritten, and any run that acts on a board must reconcile the heading against the newest run-log
entry naming that slot before trusting it.** Reconciliation this run took one grep.
**SECOND HALF OF THE LESSON, from the prune, and it is the same failure in a different register.**
The prune method (refined Sep 9) says to confirm a section's lessons are in the PRIOR LESSONS
blocks before deleting it. **That check had never actually found a gap, and today it did: there was
no lessons block for 19–23 at all — the blocks jumped 15–18 to 24–26 — and Lesson 19 existed
NOWHERE in this file except inside the Aug 20 section scheduled for deletion.** One more run and it
was gone. **19, 22 and 23 are now consolidated in their own block; 20 and 21 were never assigned.**
**The generalisable point: a verification step that has never failed has never been tested. The
prune's register check was exercised on Sep 9 and paid off; its LESSON check was exercised today
for the first time and paid off bigger.**

  but reader-facing copy against a TSB link may not.**
  **THE FIX IS ONE SENTENCE, NOT A RESEARCH TASK: print "starting 29 September" and stop.** The
  reader consequence — this gets harder to buy after the 29th — survives both cuts intact.
  **Do not swap the link-out to whitehouse.gov to rescue the two details.** TSB carries the Swonger
  quote, the 70% figure and the province context, which are worth more to a reader than a timestamp.

### `mgp-chapter-one-out-of-the-shadows-2026` — NEW, STAGED Fri Sep 18. SINGLE-SOURCED. It answers the Sep 18 ask exactly.

**Link-out: `https://www.thespiritsbusiness.com/2026/09/mgp-bottles-first-namesake-bourbon/` — TSB,
10 September, Miona Madsen. Read in full.**
**THE SEP 18 BOARD ASKED FOR ONE NON-WA ITEM, IDEALLY EXPLORER OR VENUE REGULAR. This is TSB and
Explorer.** Both current Sep 18 items are WA and both are Social Drinker; **this fixes outlet and
persona in one item**, which is the only kind of item that board was allowed to take.

**What TSB supports:** Luxco's MGP Ingredients has launched its first namesake spirit, **MGP
Chapter One: Out of the Shadows** · MGP is one of the largest American whiskey producers,
Lawrenceburg, Indiana · **it has made spirits for hundreds of brands as a third-party producer** ·
the launch coincides with **renaming the Indiana facility MGP Distillery** — previously Rossville,
Seagrams, and most recently **Ross & Squibb** · **10-year-old straight bourbon** · master distiller
**Ian Stirsman** · **high-rye LBSV mash of 60% corn, 36% rye, 4% malted barley** · barrels from
**Rickhouse G** · **55.5% ABV** · **limited quantities nationwide in the US this autumn** ·
**SRP US$69.99 for 750ml** · Julie Francis, president and CEO, quoted · and the honest context:
MGP **paused** its Kentucky whiskey distilleries (Limestone Branch, Lux Row) in May on falling
demand, with **whiskey sales down 42%** in its most recent results.

**SECOND-SOURCE HUNT RAN AND FAILED, and the failure is instructive rather than disqualifying.**
Pickups exist at BevNET (a PR wire), Beverage Information Group, StockTitan (the company's own
release), Yahoo syndication, Hi Proof and Global Drinks Intel, **and Fred Minnick ran it Sept 10.**
**Every one of them is the same press release — FM's copy repeats the release's phrasing verbatim
("bringing the legendary distiller's whiskey and spirits expertise directly to consumers").
Lesson 15/18 applies: a press release read through eight outlets is still ONE source.**
**IT RUNS FLAGGED, exactly as `sagamore-bottled-in-bond-rye-2026` does.**

**SENSORY IS BLOCKED, and this one is a clean example of the trap.** TSB states in its own voice
that the bourbon "offers aromas of butterscotch, burnt sugar, molasses, and cherry" with a palate
of "cherry cola, raspberries, chocolate, sassafras, and leather." **That identical string appears
in every pickup and in the company's own release. Nobody independent tasted it. DO NOT PRINT IT.**
**Lesson 15's standing test: ask whether the source tasted THIS bottle. No source did.**

**THE HOOK IS REPORTED, NOT SENSORY, AND IT IS A GOOD ONE.** The distillery whose whiskey has been
sold under hundreds of other people's names is putting its own name on a bottle, and taking its
old name back at the same time. **The brand's own title for it — "Out of the Shadows" — is the
story.** A reader who has ever read "distilled in Lawrenceburg, Indiana" on a back label already
knows this company without knowing it. **That is the click.**
**Beat `whats-dropping` (or `try-this-next`), persona Explorer.** **$69.99 nationwide with a
10-year age statement is a real forward-bar profile** — better than anything else staged this week.
**One fact fork, and it is minor: FM adds that the bourbon "debuts at Kentucky Bourbon Festival
this weekend" and TSB does not. TSB is the link-out, so that detail cannot be printed.**

### Urgent sweep — no override

**Ran. Nothing qualifies.** No product recall, no safety issue, no litigation or investigation
naming a partner brand or venue, and no death in the industry inside the window. **What the sweep
surfaced and correctly did not escalate:** the Diageo Cameronbridge strike (labour, not safety);
MGP's 42% whiskey-sales drop and paused Kentucky distilleries (business condition, already months
old); and the standing pre-existing items already in this file — the Bardstown HR discrimination
suit (February) and the Uncle Nearest receivership (**still recommend hold**). **Neither is new and
neither moved this run.** **Sep 9's precedent held and is now twice-applied: a trade action is not
on the urgent list, and neither is a strike.**

## WEDNESDAY Sep 9 — PREP RUN (posted 06:1x PT)

**Calendar check ran first (Lesson 51) and every board heading is correct:** Sep 9 is a Wednesday;
next Fri = **Sep 11**, next Tue = **Sep 15**, then Fri **Sep 18**, Tue **Sep 22**. All four confirmed
with `date -d`. **No slot date was copied forward from this file.**

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 52. A FACT RECORDED AS "DOES NOT EXIST" IS A CLAIM WITH A SHELF LIFE, AND THE PLACE TO RE-TEST IT IS THE COMMERCE PAGE, NOT THE NEWS PAGE.

The file said, in bold, three times: **NO MEMBERSHIP PRICE EXISTS** for the New Era club — checked on
TSB, on WKYT, and on the newerawhiskey.com homepage. **All three of those checks were correct when
they were made, and all three were the wrong place to look.** A membership price does not appear in a
trade report or on a marketing homepage; **it appears on the page where you buy the thing.** This run
found it in one search and one fetch: New Era sells the membership through a Shopify storefront that
the homepage links to directly, and that product page carries the price in its own structured data.
**The generalisable rule: when an item's payoff is an action (join, book, buy, reserve), the
re-confirm target is the commerce page, and "no price published" is only true once you have checked
it.** This is Lesson 48 ("re-confirm the page where the action happens") pushed one step further —
Lesson 48 said re-confirm the action; **Lesson 52 says the action page and the news page are different
pages, and a missing commercial fact lives on the former.**
**Second half of the same lesson, from the prune:** the prune method said "confirm keys in DEDUP," and
two Aug 19 keys were not there. **The instinct was to treat that as a blocker; the correct reading was
that DEDUP is the register for published and staged keys and `DROPPED / DO NOT REDISCOVER` is the
register for dropped ones.** Both keys were properly protected, in the right place. **A verification
step that does not know which register a fact belongs in produces false alarms — name the register,
not just the file.**

### THE SEP 11 PRE-EDITION PASS — all three link-outs re-fetched. TWO UNCHANGED, ONE MATERIALLY CHANGED.

- **`whisky-advocate-grilling-with-whisky-2026` — UNCHANGED, and the price absence is now properly

  byline, Elizabeth Karmel named as former executive chef of **New York City's Hill Country

  **NO PRICES — AND THIS TIME IT WAS TESTED PROPERLY, PER LESSON 50.** Long fetch, 77KB raw, the full

  inside scripts and JSON. **The absence is real. The item still cannot carry a price.**
  **COMPLIANCE, unchanged and load-bearing: Karmel's guidance is measured in tablespoons of whisky as
  a cooking ingredient. That is recipe volume, not drinking volume — but do not lift the quantities

  **Re-confirmed exactly as staged: 92 points · SRP $132 · ABV 53.6% · available nationwide.**

  Málaga, the only aromatized wine with a Spanish DO, macerated with Seville orange peels at least 6

  on the nose; burnt orange, candied orange peel, Jaffa cakes, plum, honey, snickerdoodles on the

- **`new-era-kentucky-distillery-club-2026` — MATERIALLY CHANGED, TWICE, AND BOTH CHANGES ARE GOOD.**
  TSB link-out re-fetched, 200, and **every staged fact still reads as staged** (ribbon cut by Zane
  Brammell on 2 September, Jeff Noel / Linda Gorton / Martina Barksdale attending, the four bourbons
  Stage Diver / Frankenfurt / Lefty / The Heart, all cask-strength single barrel, headline-style names,
  the club's private tasting sessions, one bottle per month delivered, members-only perks, the
  Covid-19 hobby origin). **Andy Beshear is also quoted on the page.**
  **CHANGE 1 — THE PRICE EXISTS NOW. `$249`.** Source: **New Era's own Shopify storefront**,
  `new-era-whiskey-2.myshopify.com/products/join-the-social-club`, 200 — **and newerawhiskey.com links
  to that exact URL, which is what makes it the brand's own page and a Tier 1 primary, not a random
  storefront.** The page states its own price in three independent places in its markup:
  `og:price:amount` = **249.00 USD**, the product variant object = **249.0 USD** (SKU
  `NewEra-Membership`, `available: true`), and the analytics payload = **"249.00"**.
  **THE BILLING PERIOD IS ALSO SETTLED, and this matters more than the number: the store carries
  exactly ONE subscription plan, named `"1 month subscription"`, at `planPrice: 24900`. So $249 is a
  MONTHLY figure, not an annual one.** **Do not print "$249 a year" under any circumstance.**
  **One honest caveat to carry forward: the widget also exposes a `One-time` purchase option
  alongside the subscription, so a reader could buy once at $249 rather than subscribe.** **Safest
  printable phrasing: "$249 a month." Do NOT write "$249 for a year of bottles."**
  **What the store page supports beyond the price:** monthly members-only bottle shipped to the door ·
  members-only single barrel picks · participation in barrel picks · private events at the distillery ·
  **access to the private club space in downtown Lexington, stated in the present tense** · email
  newsletters · members-only Facebook and Discord groups.
  **THAT PRESENT TENSE IS ITSELF A FINDING: the homepage still says "upcoming private social club
  space" and is copyrighted 2025; the store page says "Access to our private social club space" and is
  copyrighted 2026.** **The store page is the fresher artefact — prefer it on any conflict.**
  **CHANGE 2 — THE NAME COLLISION IS MUCH SMALLER THAN THE FILE THOUGHT, and Aaron needs to know
  before he rules.** **The phrase "Whiskey Social Club" appears NOWHERE** — not on the Shopify store
  page, not on newerawhiskey.com, not in the TSB piece. Grepped all three raw fetches: **zero hits.**
  **The brand's own name for it is "NEW ERA SOCIAL CLUB MEMBERSHIP" / "Social Club."** **WKYT's
  headline wording alone produced "Whiskey Social Club."** So the risk is not that a competitor has
  taken our name — **it is that one regional TV station described somebody else's club using our
  company name.** **The recommended default is therefore STRONGER than the Sep 6 default: run the
  item, call it "a members-only club," and never print the phrase "Whiskey Social Club" — which is
  now easy, because neither the link-out nor the brand uses it.**
  **STILL NOT SUPPORTED ANYWHERE — do not print: NO TASTING NOTES EXIST.** Sensory stays blocked
  outright; print the construction (cask strength, single barrel) and never the taste. **The street
  address 333 E. Short Street still comes only from search summaries — it surfaced again in this
  run's search and is still not printable. "East Short Street near Thoroughbred Park" is what WKYT
  supports.**
  **THE STORE PAGE IS THICK WITH UNUSABLE COPY — none of it may be lifted:** "you're joining a
  movement" · "May your glass always be full and your problems empty" (**a volume cue — hard no**) ·
  and the homepage's journey / premium / experience / craftsmanship / toast pile.
  **THE LINK-OUT DECISION, AND IT IS A REAL ONE FOR THURSDAY OR FRIDAY.** One link per item, and every
  printed claim must be traceable to it. **TSB supports the opening, the officials, the bourbons and
  the club's shape but NOT the price. The store page supports the price and the join action but NOT
  the Sept 2 opening.** So: **print the price → link the store page and drop the opening date.
  Print the opening → link TSB and drop the price.** **RECOMMENDATION: keep TSB as the link-out and
  drop the price from copy.** Reasons: TSB is the better read, it is the neutral wording on the name
  question, a Last Call venue item wants the "it's open, officials came, here are the bottles" frame,
  and **$249 a month is a steep number to put in front of a reader as a recommendation — printing it
  against a storefront link makes the item read as a pitch for someone else's subscription.** **If
  Aaron would rather the price ran, the link-out must move to the store page and the item becomes a
  "here is what it costs" item, not a "here is a place that opened" item.**

### `canada-spirits-import-ban-2026` — NEW KEY, VERIFIED ON A TIER 1 PRIMARY + TSB, STAGED Fri Sep 11 as the fourth item.

**THIS IS A NEW ACTION ON A COVERED STORY, AND IT CLEARS THE DEDUP EXCEPTION CLEANLY.**
`canada-50pct-tariff-2026` **PUBLISHED Sep 4 and stays closed.** This is not a restatement, a
reminder or a countdown — **it is a new presidential proclamation, signed after that edition ran, that
replaces a tariff with a prohibition and carries its own effective date.** That is precisely the
"a rate changed / a government acted" case the exception exists for. **NEWLY KEYED rather than
strengthened, because the instrument itself changed: Sep 4 covered a 50% duty; this is an import ban.**
**Any copy must open with what is new and may reference the earlier coverage in one clause only.**

**Link-out `thespiritsbusiness.com/2026/09/trump-bans-canadian-spirits-imports/` (TSB, 9 September,
Joe Rogers).** 200. **Second source, and it is the primary: the White House proclamation itself**,
`whitehouse.gov/presidential-actions/2026/09/excluding-certain-canadian-alcoholic-beverages-from-importation-into-the-united-states-...`,
200. **Tier 1 primary plus one Tier 2 corroborating trade report is exactly what the two-source rule
asks for — this is not two reprints of one release.**

**Supported by BOTH:** the proclamation was signed/issued **Tuesday 8 September 2026** · it excludes
certain Canadian alcoholic beverages from importation into the US · **effective 12:01 a.m. eastern
time on 29 September 2026** · the affected goods were already subject to the 50% import tariff ·
**goods imported before Sept 29 remain subject to the 50% duty rate** · the stated justification is
"continued discrimination against the commerce of the United States with respect to alcoholic
beverages."
**Supported by TSB only:** the categories as **"spirits, wine, and malt beer"** · TSB's own
characterisation "near-total ban" · **Chris Swonger, president and CEO of DISCUS**, on the record ·
US spirits exports to Canada **fell by more than 70%** · eight of Canada's ten provinces removed US
alcohol from sale in early 2025 · a July survey putting **69%** of Canadians in favour of the
restrictions · Pernod Ricard (JP Wiser) and Diageo reporting increased Canadian sales.
**Supported by the proclamation only — richer, and useful for accuracy rather than for copy:**
Proclamation **11046** of July 20 2026 imposed the duties effective Aug 19 · Proclamation **11056** of
Aug 18 suspended them 3 days · **Canada reneged Aug 21 and the duties took effect 12:01 a.m. Aug 22**
(this confirms the file's "live since Saturday" note was right) · **Saskatchewan announced an
additional 50% levy on US alcohol effective September 8** — which is what the file's earlier
"Canada's answer has a date: September 8" actually turned out to be · the authority is section 338 of
the Tariff Act of 1930 · the specific goods sit in an **Annex**.

**THE QUOTE, IF ONE IS WANTED — one clean option, named, Tier 2, under the cap:** Swonger's
**"For more than a year and a half, American distillers have shouldered the brunt of this trade
dispute"** — **17 words**, counted. **Only usable if TSB is the link-out.** No quote is needed; the
item works without one.
**DO NOT PRINT: a bottle-level list of banned products.** TSB says spirits, wine and malt beer; **the
detail sits in an Annex this run did not parse, so do not name specific brands or bottlings as banned.**
**Do not print the search-summary line about "beer, wine, whisky, bourbon, rum, vodka" — that came
from a search summary, not from either source.**
**SLOT REASONING — Sep 11, and it satisfies the board's own gate.** The Sep 11 board's standing rule
was "do NOT add a fourth unless it is neither WA nor a venue." **A TSB trade-policy item is neither.**
It also **fixes the board's one recorded weakness**, outlet concentration. **Precedent for running it
in a Last Call exists and should be followed exactly: the Sep 4 edition ran the tariff item LAST,
factual, with no payoff line and no occasion framing.** **Beat `whats-dropping` is wrong for it; the
Sep 4 precedent and the honest read is that this is a shelf-and-wallet item — `try-this-next` is also
wrong. It has no clean beat slug, which is a real gap (see OPEN GAPS) — the closest is
`allocation-watch` in the sense of "availability is about to change," and that is what to use.**
Persona **Explorer**.
**FALLBACK: Tue Sep 15 also works and is not stale** — the ban bites Sept 29, so a Sep 15 run is still
two weeks ahead of it. **Sep 15 needs material more than Sep 11 does.** **But Sep 11 is the better

### `whisky-advocate-new-zealand-single-malts-2026` — NEW, VERIFIED, STAGED Tue Sep 22. THIS CLOSES THE SEP 22 ASK EXACTLY.

**The Sep 22 instruction was "find a third and fourth from a NON-RR outlet, ideally Explorer/value —
do not add more Collector items and do not add more RR." This item is WA, Explorer, and carries a
$75 bottle.** Off the WA RSS feed, first read of the run (Lesson 34 held again).

**Link-out `whiskyadvocate.com/new-zealand-whisky-distilleries` (WA, September 8, Julia Higgins).**
200. **VERIFIED, one source by design — WA reported it and WA tasted it, so WA is the primary.**
Beat `try-this-next`, persona Explorer.

**Supported, and printable:** New Zealand went from **zero single malt distilleries at the start of
the millennium to 20 and counting** · total barrelage is still tiny and only a few distillers export ·
**laureate barley is the shared grain**, and it grows sweeter in NZ than in Scotland · longer
fermentations and slow distillations, what Cardrona's Elsom calls an "unhurried approach" (Cardrona
around 115 hours historically, closer to 72 now; Pōkeno ~90 hours) · **angels' share runs 9.5% a year
on North Island against 3.5% at Cardrona on South Island**, which is why South Island will reach older
age statements · Pōkeno built **the country's only cooperage**, making barrels from native totara and
kauri · **manuka wood is used to smoke malt** by Pōkeno and Scapegrace · The New Zealand Whisky
Collection oxidised **local pinot noir casks** rather than importing sherry butts · **Scapegrace is
distributed in a handful of US states through Sazerac's 375 Park Avenue arm** · Cardrona was bought by
**International Beverage in 2023** · Pōkeno's capacity is 66,000 gallons against Buffalo Trace's
estimated 2.65 million.
**WA'S OWN SCORED NOTES — the whole reason this item is Explorer-grade:**
**Pōkeno Discovery, 91 points, 43%, $75** — raisins, black cherry, orange peel, malt, vanilla, milk
chocolate, toffee, black pepper. **THIS IS THE VALUE ANCHOR AND THE ONE TO LEAD THE BLURB ON.**
**Cardrona The Falcon, 92 points, 52%, $285.** Vanilla pudding, orange zest, cinnamon, cardamom,
clove, sweet custard, tannic oak.
**Scapegrace Anthem, NR, 40%, $90 — "NR" means not rated and NO NOTES ARE PUBLISHED for it. Do not
describe how Anthem tastes.**
**NAMED SPEAKERS on the page:** Sarah Elsom (Cardrona head distiller) · Matt Johns (Pōkeno founder) ·
Michael Byars (The New Zealand Whisky Collection GM and head distiller) · Mark Neal (Scapegrace
co-founder). **Cleanest short quote if one is wanted: Elsom's "We have a fantastic climate and
fertile soils for barley here" — 11 words, named, Tier 2.**
**TERMS NEEDING A PLAIN-ENGLISH CLAUSE ON FIRST USE: "single malt" and "angels' share."**
**DO NOT PRINT: any US availability claim beyond Scapegrace's "handful of states."** The three prices
are in USD, which implies US pricing, **but the piece never says the other two are sold nationwide —
do not upgrade that.** **Do not reuse the article's own "hunt for barrels" phrasing carelessly; "hunt"
is capped at once per edition.**

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 51. A BOARD LABEL IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE CALENDAR, AND IT DECAYS LIKE ANY OTHER CLAIM.

**The Sep 6 run staged three verified items to "Fri Sep 18." The next Friday was Sep 11.** Sep 7
propagated the error without re-deriving it ("Sep 15 and Sep 18 closed"), and this run inherited a
file in which **the next edition had no board at all** — three days out, on the slot that is already
the hardest to fill.
**Nothing about the items was wrong. The label was wrong.** Every downstream artefact — the OPEN GAPS
entry, the Slack PIPELINE lines on Sep 6 and Sep 7, the "next prep" arithmetic — was internally
consistent and pointed at the wrong week.
**The rule: derive the slot date from today's date every run, never copy it forward from the file.**
Lesson 48 said a payoff decays before its facts do. **Lesson 51 is narrower and cheaper to obey: a
DATE decays the moment the week turns, and a date carried forward is an unverified claim.**
**Concrete check, one command, every run: compute the next Tuesday and the next Friday from today in
America/Los_Angeles, and confirm every open board matches one of them.** This run did it with
`date -d <YYYY-MM-DD> +%A` on all five forward dates. **It costs one shell call and it caught a
missed edition.**

edition-day re-confirm in this file that forced **no** change, and the reason is structural: the Sep 7

### Voice and compliance, checked before send

- **ONE verbatim quote in the edition** — O'Driscoll, 9 words, named speaker, from the Tier 2 page
  that item links to. **Op den Kamp's quote was deliberately NOT used.** His shortest usable line is
  16 words and clears the cap, but the edition cap is one quote and O'Driscoll's does more work — it
  tells the reader chinquapin is not exotic. **The 20-word Op den Kamp line still fails the cap.**
- **Banned words: clean in WS's own voice.** **"toasted" appears twice and both are permitted lifts** —
  "toasted nuts" inside WA's attributed tasting note, and "the toasted barrel category" inside a claim
  explicitly attributed to WA, where it is the category's actual name. **Same proper-noun logic that
  permits "Buffalo Trace Antique Collection."** **"Collection" was avoided entirely** — the Johnnie
  Walker item was written so the sale's formal name was never needed.
- **"Hunt" used ZERO times.** The WA page uses it twice (in Op den Kamp's quote and in its closing
  line); neither was lifted.
- **DISCUS 73.8 clean** — no volume, rate or intoxication cue anywhere. "You will not need a huge
  budget to bid" is a cost statement, not a consumption one.
- **Sentence lengths 3–15 throughout**; two drafts were split to get there. No exclamation points.
  Sentence-case bolded headlines.
- **Both sensory-blocked items say so to the reader, in copy, unhedged.** Blue Note: "No independent
  tasting is published yet." Michter's: "No independent tasting is published, so the flavor is still
  an open question." **Two of four items admitting the flavor is unverified is not a weakness of the
  edition — it is the sourced-claims rule visible on the page.**

## MONDAY Sep 7 — PREP RUN (posted 06:15 PT)

**Window Sep 6 – Sep 7 inclusive. Job was (1) the Tue Sep 8 pre-edition pass and (2) Tue Sep 22, the
only open board — and both closed.** No fifth item was added to Sep 8, per instruction 2. Sep 15 got
no new Collector items and Sep 18 was neither raided nor re-researched, per instructions 5 and 6.

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 50. A PAGE THE PIPELINE HAS ALREADY "READ" CAN STILL BE HOLDING THE FACT A BOARD SAYS IS MISSING.

**`whisky-advocate-chinquapin-oak-2026` has been staged since Aug 27 with the standing note "No price
on the page — print none." That note was wrong.** The page ends in a **"Chinquapin Whiskies to Try"**
box carrying six bottles with scores, ABVs, prices and WA's own four-descriptor tasting notes on each.
It was missed because the Aug 27 fetch was short and the box sits at the bottom — **the exact failure
mode Sep 6 identified for WA *reviews* and fixed by fetching 12,000+ chars. It applies to WA
*features* too, and to any WA page.**
**The rule: when a board note says a fact does not exist on a page, re-fetch the page LONG before the
edition, not on the strength of the note.** A missing price is a claim about a page, and claims about
pages decay exactly like claims about products (Lesson 48).
**This is Lesson 49's twin.** Lesson 49 said work the bench before opening a seam. Lesson 50 says
**work the page you already have before you trust its absence.** Both are the same economy: the
cheapest fact is the one already inside something you fetched.

### `basil-hayden-cognac-cask-reserve-2026` — CONVERTED. VERIFIED, STAGED Tue Sep 22. The value/Explorer item the boards keep wanting.

**Two independent vetted sources.** **Link-out is ROBB REPORT** (Jonah Flicker, Sept 3,
`basil-haydencognac-cask-reserve-bourbon-1238621307`), **second source WA's Sept 4 Whisky Watch.**

**Why RR and not WA — this is the whole point of the conversion.** The Sep 6 run flagged that **WA's
entry prints "ABV: $40," a self-evidently corrupt field**, and technique (j) forbids repairing it from
another page while WA is the link-out. **RR states the ABV in its own editorial voice** — Basil Hayden
"is always bottled at 80 proof, the minimum level legally required for American whiskey" — **so with
RR as the link-out the proof is printable and the corrupt-field problem disappears.** **Do not print
40% and 80 proof both; RR's framing is proof, so print 80 proof.**

**What RR supports, and it is more than a reprint does:** SRP **$50**; **"available starting this
month from select retailers"** plus Total Wine; **finished in French oak casks that previously held
cognac for 20 to 25 years**; **no age statement** (RR's own estimate of six to eight years is RR's
inference — **do not print it as fact**); the Small Batch line context (Booker's, Baker's, Knob
Creek); **Freddie Noe named as eighth-generation master distiller.**
**RR'S OWN SENSORY IS PRINTABLE, attributed to Robb Report:** "signature Jim Beam nut and toasted oak
flavors, as well notes of vanilla, caramel, spiced fruit, and apple cinnamon on the palate."
**THE BEST DETAIL IN THE PIECE IS REPORTED, NOT SENSORY: RR asked how long the secondary maturation
lasted and the brand would not say**, offering a non-answer statement instead. **That is the hook —
a real reported fact, it is WS-voice-safe, and no reprint has it.**
**What WA adds independently:** SRP $50, **"available at retailers nationwide"** despite being a
limited release, the signature high-rye mashbill, **"the first time that Basil Hayden has brought an
international finish into the fold."** **Nationwide is WA's word and it is the forward-bar fact —
name WA inside the sentence if it is printed (technique a).**
**DO NOT USE:** the Suntory release, PR Newswire, Yahoo Finance, The Bourbon Flight (**not vetted**),
The Manual, Creative Brands, Northwest Beer Guide, The Whisky Wire. **All are one origin with the
release, and the release's tasting notes are brand copy.**
Beat `try-this-next`, persona **Explorer**. **$50 and nationwide is the profile the last four boards
have been short of.**
Link: `https://robbreport.com/food-drink/spirits/basil-hayden-cognac-cask-reserve-bourbon-1238621307/`

## SUNDAY Sep 6 — PREP RUN (posted 06:14 PT)

**Window Sep 4 – Sep 6 inclusive, three days, no Saturday run. Job was FRI SEP 18 plus two carried
checks — and all of it closed.** Sep 8 was not researched and no fifth item was added, per
instruction 4. Sep 15 got no new Collector items, per instruction 5.

### THE RUN'S LESSON — Lesson 49. A BENCH ITEM CAN BE THE ANSWER TO A SHAPE GAP, AND IT IS CHEAPER THAN DISCOVERY.

**Instruction 3 asked for a value/what-to-try Explorer item for the third Sep 18 slot, and warned
that a third Explorer-thin Friday would earn a Notes bullet.** The reflex is to go hunting a new
seam. **The answer was already in the file:** `deanston-17-orange-wine-cask-2026` had been sitting on
the UNASSESSED bench since Sep 3 with the note "WA's own sensory; needs price + US distribution."

**One fetch resolved BOTH blockers, because both live in the same review box at the bottom of the
page — the part the Sep 3 skim never reached.** SRP $132, "available nationwide," 92 points, plus
WA's full tasting note. **The gap closed for the cost of one page.**

**THE GENERALISABLE RULE: before opening a new seam for a SHAPE gap (persona, beat, price band),
re-read the unassessed bench against that shape.** A bench item's blocker is often a fact that was
never actually looked for, not a fact that does not exist. **And when a WA review's blockers are
"price" and "distribution," they are almost always in the review box — fetch the FULL page, not the
first few thousand characters.** This run's first Deanston fetch stopped at 4,000 chars and missed
the box; the second one at 12,000 found it.

### `whisky-advocate-grilling-with-whisky-2026` — VERIFIED, STAGED Fri Sep 18. One source by design.

**Link-out `whiskyadvocate.com/grilling-with-whisky-recipes` (WA, Sept 3, Aubrey Thompson byline —
same byline as the Sep 4 crumble bars; pronouns still not stated anywhere, so attribute to the outlet
or use they/them).** 200, 9,866 chars of body.

**ONE SOURCE BY DESIGN, exactly like the crumble bars — a Tier 2 outlet's own editorial with its own
expert. NEVER log it as needing a second source.**

**Beat `pair-it`, persona Social Drinker.** The expert is **Elizabeth Karmel**, named and credentialed
in the piece: **former executive chef of New York City's Hill Country Barbecue, consultant, cookbook
author, grilling authority, self-described bourbon lover.**

**WHAT THE PAGE SUPPORTS, and it is a lot:**
- **Four recipes, one each in cocktail, main dish, sauce and dessert** — the piece says exactly that.
- **Karmel's method:** start with **one to two tablespoons** in most recipes, more only for marinades,
  where the greater volume also tenderizes. Cook with something you enjoy drinking. **She gravitates
  toward softer whiskies that emphasise flavour over heat and tends to avoid heavily smoky
  expressions.**
- **The genuinely useful safety line:** never pour whisky directly onto food over an open flame or lit
  grill; glazes and sauces are safe because the spirit is diluted; apply with a long-handled brush.
- **Named bottles:** 2XO American Oak straight bourbon · WhistlePig Piggyback rye · Kilbeggan Irish
  whiskey · Indri Trini single malt · Maker's Mark and Laphroaig in the two anecdotes.
- **SENSORY EXISTS AND IS ATTRIBUTABLE TO A NAMED PERSON:** Maker's Mark was the only one of a dozen
  bourbons she tested with the **"full-bodied, intense vanilla flavor"** she wanted for a sweet potato
  mash · Indri Trini's triple-cask maturation **"brings notes of caramelized pineapple, black tea, and
  toasted almond"** (Karmel, direct) · Kilbeggan chosen for its **"lighter profile"** · a splash of
  Laphroaig gave American-smoked salmon the salinity she remembered from Scotland.
- **Quote candidate, 12 words, named speaker, from the link-out:** *"It added a terroir-specific smoky
  element that took it from good to extraordinary."*

**WHAT THE PAGE DOES NOT SUPPORT — and this is the item's one real weakness: THERE ARE NO PRICES.
Not one, on any of the named bottles.** **So this item CANNOT carry a price, and it is not the
crumble-bars pattern** (that item printed a $45 bottle). **Do not go find a price elsewhere and print
it against this link-out — that violates Lesson 39.**

**FOUR HARD CONSTRAINTS ON DRAFTING THIS, all checked this run:**
1. **PINEAPPLE IS SPENT THROUGH SEPTEMBER and the dessert recipe is grilled pineapple.** **The
   dessert must not be the hook.** The Sep 6 prep post said "cocktail to dessert" and never named it.
2. **COCONUT IS FLAGGED FIVE WAYS and the same dessert carries toasted coconut.** Second reason the
   dessert stays unnamed.
3. **"SPIKED WITH" IS BANNED AND THE PAGE USES IT** — "this whiskey-spiked take." **Never lift it.**
   Also avoid Karmel's "not so much that it slaps you in the face" and the "high-alcohol content can
   easily flare up" line as reader copy — the safety point can be made without the alcohol framing.
4. **"FINEST" IS BANNED AND THE PAGE USES IT** — "no need to reach for your finest." **Never lift it.**
   The idea is fine and printable in WS words: cook with what you like, save the good stuff for the glass.

**THE COCKTAIL-COUNT CALL, MADE AND SURFACED.** The piece contains a cocktail (a bourbon Arnold Palmer
built on grilled lemons). **The WA cocktail count stands at FOUR and a fifth needs Aaron's say-so.**
**This run's default: the item is FOOD, not a cocktail item — its beat is `pair-it` and the cocktail
is one of four recipes.** **So the count stays at four, and the Sep 6 Notes bullet told Aaron the call
was made.** **If he says otherwise, the marinade and compound butter carry the item alone and the
cocktail is simply not mentioned.**

### `four-roses-le-small-batch-2026` — SECOND SOURCE LANDED. Now VERIFIED. The flag is retired.

**Instruction 5 permitted exactly one check and it paid off on the standing rail.** **WA's Whisky Watch,
Sept 4** — `whiskyadvocate.com/four-roses-bakers-13-ardbeg-trudernish-and-more-new-whisky`, 200,
12,844 chars.

**IT IS INDEPENDENT EDITORIAL, NOT A RELEASE REPRINT — and that is why it counts.** Four staff bylines
(Julia Higgins, Danny Brandon, Jonny McCormick, Aubrey Thompson), WA's own framing ("the art of the
blend is on full display this week"), and **WA's own component-by-component breakdown that the FM

## FRIDAY Sep 4 — WHAT SHIPPED (Last Call, posted 06:35 PT)

**Four items, four beats, four distinct outlets. Zero quotes. Send-off "Worth the pour. See you
Tuesday."** Order as shipped: Shortbarrel (`whats-dropping`) · Buffalo Trace On Tour
(`on-the-calendar`) · WA crumble bars (`pair-it`) · Canadian whisky slump (`try-this-next`, last,
factual, no payoff line, as the Sep 2 file directed).

### THE EDITION-DAY RE-CONFIRM CHANGED THE EDITION — and this is the run's lesson

**Lesson 48. A staged item can die, or invert, in the 48 hours between the last prep run and the
edition — and the thing that inverts is usually the PAYOFF, not the facts.** Every fact in the
Buffalo Trace entry was still true this morning. The dates, cities, session counts and 21+ rule had
not moved a comma. **What moved was the only thing that made it worth a reader's Friday: you could
act on it.** Three of four stops filled inside 48 hours.

**Two prep runs in a row had this item as "the right Sep 4 lead: reservations open Sept 2 and the
edition lands Sept 4 — the reader can act the moment they read it."** That was true when written and
false by publication. **Nothing short of re-fetching the primary on the morning of the edition would
have caught it.** The Sep 3 file's instruction 3(b) — re-read the WA page and confirm the reservation
line — was necessary but would NOT have been sufficient: **WA's page still reads "Reservations open
September 2 at 10 a.m. ET" and always will. It is a dated news note, not a live status.**

**THE GENERALISABLE RULE: when an item's payoff is "you can act on this now," the edition-day
re-confirm must hit the page WHERE THE ACTION HAPPENS, not the outlet that reported it.** A ticket
page, a booking page, a DTC store. **The trade press reports an opening; only the primary reports a
closing.** Add this to any future staged item whose value is availability.

### `buffalo-trace-on-tour-2026` — LINK-OUT MOVED TO THE PRIMARY. Deliberate, and here is the reasoning.

**Fetched `buffalotracedistillery.com/on-tour/` under the browser UA, 200, server-rendered.**
Stop-by-stop status as of this morning:
- **Branson, MO — Oct 1–4 — OPEN.** No "full" label. The only bookable stop.
- **Kansas City, MO — Oct 8–11 — "Reservations are full."**
- **Dallas/Ft. Worth, TX — Oct 14–17 — "Reservations are full."**
- **Denver, CO — Nov 12–15 — "Reservations are full."**
- All three carry **"Continue to check back as additional reservations may open."**

**THE LINK-OUT MOVED FROM WA TO THE BT PRIMARY, and the file should not re-litigate it.** Three
reasons, in order: (a) **the sold-out status lives ONLY on the primary** — under Lesson 39 the claim
must live in the link-out, and a WA link with a printed "three stops are full" would have violated
it; (b) **the primary is where the reader books** — the payoff is now "book Branson," and WA cannot
be booked from; (c) **precedent exists** — the Aug 25 Shortlist linked `garrisonbros.com` directly
for an event, for the same reason. **WA and WKYT drop to corroboration.** The story key is unchanged,
so **no dedup consequence**.

**It also lost the lead.** Shortbarrel's DTC drop was the edition day itself and became the lead;
Buffalo Trace ran second. **A mostly-full tour is honest and still useful — it is not a lead.**

**Printed, and all of it from the BT page:** reservations opened Wednesday · the three full stops
named · Branson still open Oct 1–4 · sessions guided · 21 and over · check back as more open · book
on its own tour page · third-party reservations do not count. **NOT printed: the pour count ("four
tasting pours" is on the page and is a DISCUS 73.8 trap), "complimentary" (cut deliberately — it
reads as free drinks; the booking framing replaced it), "Sazerac Rye 100 Proof" (WKYT's, not the
link-out's), and every instance of "journey"/"experience"/"Collections" from the BT page.**

### `canada-50pct-tariff-2026` — LINK-OUT SWAP ADOPTED, QUOTE DROPPED, AND ONE CLAIM DID NOT SURVIVE THE MOVE

**Link-out is now `thespiritsbusiness.com/2026/09/canadian-whisky-slumps-in-us-control-states/`
(Nicola Carruthers, Sept 1). Re-fetched, 200, full body.**

**THE CARNEY QUOTE IS DROPPED.** It lives in the Aug 24 piece, the link-out moved, and a quote must
come from the linked source. **The Sep 3 file called this exactly. The edition ran zero quotes and is
no worse for it — a quote is optional in this format and the cap is a ceiling, not a target.**

**A CLAIM WAS LOST IN THE MOVE, AND THE RUN CAUGHT IT. Read this before re-staging the tariff.**
The Sep 2 file recorded that "TSB states outright: the US 50% tariff includes Canadian goods such as
alcohol." **That sentence is in the AUG 24 piece. It is NOT in the Sept 1 slump piece.** The slump
piece says only "a 50% US tariff placed on Canadian products." **So the shipped item could NOT say
the tariff covers Canadian whisky, and it did not.** It printed the tariff and the sales decline as
two adjacent facts and asserted no causation — which is also all TSB does. **The trap generalises:
a link-out swap inside one outlet still moves the evidence base. Re-check every printed claim against
the NEW page, not the old entry.**

**BOTH DIRECTIONAL TRAPS HELD IN THE SHIPPED COPY.** The delisting is written as a removal from
shelves ("US alcohol has been pulled from all but two Canadian provinces"), never as a tariff. **The
Sept 8 Canadian counter-tariff was not mentioned at all** — the slump piece says only that Canada is
"planning similar action on US products," which is too loose to print safely next to a whiskey story.
**Omitting it was the right call and should be repeated.**

**Printed, all from the slump piece:** control-state definition · NABCA July data across 18 markets ·
Canadian whisky −8.5% volume, the steepest drop of any category · Scotch +3.2%, Tequila +3.1% ·
the 50% US tariff on Canadian goods · US alcohol pulled from all but two provinces since March 2025.
**Not printed:** the −8.4% value figure, the 12-month numbers, American whiskey −3.6% (all true, all
cut for length), and **"world-class" from the MBLL quote — banned, never lifted.**

**212 barrels**, third floor of Warehouse B (Brown-Forman's oldest Shively warehouse) · selected by

credits for a "sweet and fruity character") · **MSRP $199.99** · **released Sept 2** · **SWEEPSTAKES
ONLY — no in-store distillery sale; the distillery told buyers not to travel for it.**

cannot buy it at retail.** BUT Birthday Bourbon is the canonical allocated hunt — `allocation-watch`
exists for exactly this, and the Collector persona is served by the specifics. **Frame as a record/hunt,
not a "buy this."** **SENSORY IS BRAND-ATTRIBUTED — "sweet and fruity" is the distillery's, and the full
flavour string ("baking spice, ginger, vanilla, honey, graham cracker...") reads as Old Forester's own
tasting notes.** RR's body carried production detail, **not an independent RR tasting.** **Print no taste
as ours; if RR published no independent note by Sep 15, say "no independent tasting published."**

### `bruichladdich-octomore-17th-2026` — NEW, STAGED Tue Sep 15, VERIFIED (WA + TSB). US distribution confirmed; the queued distribution question is answered.

**Whisky Advocate, Danny Brandon, Sept 3, 2026** (`https://whiskyadvocate.com/octomore-17-details`),
corroborated by **The Spirits Business** (`bruichladdich-challenges-industry-norms-with-17th-octomore-line`,

Concerto barley peated to 81 PPM, 5yr first-fill bourbon, 59.7% ABV. **17.2:** same distillate, 4yr in
ex-California-red-wine French oak briefly used for bourbon, +1yr first-fill port barriques, 59.5%.
**17.3:** single-field Octomore Farm barley, highest peat at 132.1 PPM, first-fill bourbon/Ribera del
Duero/sauternes/ice wine/syrah, 60.9%. **17.4:** **the first ".4" ever released in the US** (prior ones
were distillery exclusives or abroad) — 108.2 PPM, **7yr (oldest in the lineup)**, first-fill oloroso +

## WEDNESDAY Sep 2 — PREP RUN

**Window Sep 1–2, with the WA RSS read back through Aug 31.** The run did exactly what the Sep 1 file
ordered: adopted the browser-UA fetch before any discovery, re-ran the whole discovery order under it,
and recorded the reachability answer. **That answer changed the pipeline's picture of its own supply.**

### LESSON 47 — LESSON 46 WAS RIGHT, AND THE COST OF THE BLOCKED FETCHER WAS FOUR DAYS AND A FALSE SCARCITY NARRATIVE

**All four rails, browser UA, this morning:**

| Rail | Result |
|---|---|
| `whiskyadvocate.com/call/blogs/rss/` | **200**, valid RSS, 10 items |
| `robbreport.com/food-drink/spirits/` | **200**, 389KB, 9 article URLs |
| `thespiritsbusiness.com/category/news/` | **200**, 152KB, 23 article URLs |
| `fredminnick.com` | **200**, 126KB, recent-articles rail intact |

**Every single one of the three rails the Aug 30/31 files recorded as dead was alive.** The WA RSS —
logged as four consecutive 503s and called an outage — **was carrying four items that had never been
assessed**, one of which is now staged for Fri Sep 4. The Robb Report spirits index — logged as
"307 to tollbit, monthly only, next due September" — **loads normally and renders nine articles**,
one of which is the Michter's second source this pipeline spent two runs failing to find and then
formally closed the hunt on. The Spirits Business index — one 403, treated as a wall — **loads
normally.**

**WHAT THIS COSTS AND WHAT IT TEACHES:**
- **Fri Sep 4 was never short.** It was recorded as short across four runs. The supply to fill it
  existed the whole time, on rails the pipeline had marked dead. **The Aug 31 "dry run" and the
  Aug 30 "every remaining seam tried" conclusion are both void.**
- **Lesson 45 ("never call the supply dry") and Lesson 46 (browser UA) are now the same lesson, and
  it generalises past HTTP codes: a negative discovery result is only as good as the instrument.**
  Before recording an absence, prove the instrument works. **The browser-UA fetch is now step zero
  of every run, not a fallback.**
- **The `f.sh` fetch-and-strip helper is the standard tool.** curl with the Chrome UA and `-L`, then
  strip `<script>`/`<style>`/`<svg>`, prefer the `<article>` element, strip tags, unescape entities,
  collapse whitespace. It read every page this run cleanly, including the two the pipeline believed
  were gated.
- **The do-not-retest list must never again accept a 403, 503 or 307 as evidence.** Both entries that
  came off it under Lesson 46 are now confirmed working and are **permanently off**.
